Determining the applicability of [laws of war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_war) to militant groups is a difficult question, as both the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe) and [International Committee of the Red Cross](5023832.md) note that [international law](8195726.md) treats war and terrorism as separate legal categories.[^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347] The Israeli, American, EU, UK, Japanese, and Canadian governments designate Hamas as a terrorist organization. Of other states and international bodies, some apply the designation only to its military wing, others do not designate Hamas at all, and it receives open support from Qatar and Turkey.[^cite_note-348][^cite_note-349] While the term "international law" pertains to states, it also applies to insurgent and paramilitary forces. Even if an insurgency is deemed lawful — meaning it meets the criteria of "just cause", it must adhere to the principles of "just means". Regarding Hamas and its combatants, even if they have a presumptive right to fight against what they term as an "[Israeli occupation](2781576.md#Gaza_Strip)," they must still abide by legal rules of "discrimination", "proportionality", and "military necessity".[^cite_note-baed-350]

On 9 October 2023 [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) stated that Hamas's apparent targeting of civilians, indiscriminate attacks, and taking of hostages amounted to war crimes.[^cite_note-HRW11-13]

On 10 October 2023 the [OHCHR](22463.md) stated the taking of hostages and use of human shields were war crimes.[^cite_note-ohchr-3] United Nations Human Rights chief [Volker Türk](71721726.md) noted that militant groups' "horrifying mass killings" were violations of international law.[^cite_note-UN11-2]

### Massacres

See also: [Executions and assassinations during the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executions_and_assassinations_during_the_Gaza_war) and [Allegations of genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_October_7_Hamas-led_attack_on_Israel)



Scene from an Israeli home in the aftermath of the [Be'eri massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Be'eri_massacre) carried out by Hamas



Blood on a crib in a child's room after Hamas-led attacks in Israeli communities on 7 October 2023

On 12 October, [Jens David Ohlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_David_Ohlin) argued Hamas's attacks potentially violated Articles 6-8 of the [Rome Statute](68762.md).[^cite_note-OJ-Ohlin-351] Ohlin asserted the attacks might violate Article 6, if it could be proved the perpetrators had "genocidal intent".[^cite_note-OJ-Ohlin-351] On 7 October, as his forces were massacring civilians, [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md), the head of the Hamas Political Bureau, stated in a speech the intent of [Operation Al-Aqsa Flood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Al-Aqsa_Flood), saying, "We want to liberate our land, our holy sites, our [Al-Aqsa Mosque](65759.md), our prisoners." He concluded with the statement "To \[the enemy], we have only one thing to say to you: get out of our land. Get out of our sight. Get out of our city of Al-Quds \[Jerusalem] and our al-Aqsa mosque. We no longer wish to see you on this land. This land is ours, Al-Quds is ours, everything \[here] is ours. You are strangers in this pure and blessed land. There is no place of safety for you."[^cite_note-352]

According to Ohlin, militant groups violated Article 7 if there was evidence the attacks had been part of an organized "plan or policy".[^cite_note-OJ-Ohlin-351] Finally, Ohlin argued, the militant groups violated Article 8 by killing civilians.[^cite_note-OJ-Ohlin-351] On 7 October, the militant groups [killed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_engagements_during_the_Gaza_war) civilians.[^cite_note-353] 260 were killed at the [Nova music festival](75007485.md), 112 at [Be'eri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Be'eri_massacre), and 73 at [Kfar Aza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Aza_massacre).[^cite_note-ft20231013-345] Victims were allegedly subject to immolation, dismemberment, and beheading.[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-israelhayom20231007-355] On 15 October, a group of 100 Israeli and international law experts argued videos released on social media showed war crimes.[^cite_note-Winer2023-356][^cite_note-357][^cite_note-theatlantic20231009-358] In early December 2023, ICC chief prosecutor [Karim Ahmad Khan](11606957.md) visited Israel in response to a request from a group representing families of victims of the 7 October Hamas attack on Israel. He said that the attacks against Israeli civilians on 7 October constituted some of the most serious international crimes that shock the conscience of humanity—crimes that the ICC was established to address. Khan emphasized the crimes were not random murders," and noted that Hamas "hunted down people" and that "children were kidnapped from their cots". He added that his office is willing to cooperate with Israel in investigating the events of October 7.[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360]

At the Nova music festival massacre, Hamas militants killed 270 civilians, methodically shooting fleeing and hiding attendees, as well as taking hostages, in the largest terror attack against a concert in history. Witnesses reported that some of the women were raped.[^cite_note-361][^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364]

At the Be'eri massacre, approximately 70 Hamas militants killed at least 130 people, approximately 10% of the population of [Be'eri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Be'eri), including women, children, and one[^cite_note-haaretz-false-stories-365][^cite_note-366] infant.[^cite_note-israelhayom20231007-355] When inside the kibbutz they went house to house, shooting or capturing the residents.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368]

### Hostage taking

Main article: [Gaza war hostage crisis](75025451.md)



A Palestinian militant kidnapping a civilian during the [Re'im music festival massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Re'im_music_festival_massacre) that left 378 people dead and 44 others taken hostage

Hostage-taking is outlawed in non-international armed conflicts as per Article 1(b) of Common Article 3 of the Geneva Conventions and customary international humanitarian law, and is recognized as a war crime under the Rome Statute of the International Criminal Court. Hostage-taking is defined as detaining a person with threats to harm them to compel a third party to act or abstain from acting as a condition for the hostage's safety. Hostages can be civilians or individuals not actively partaking in hostilities, including surrendered or detained armed forces members.[^cite_note-hrw1-369]

[Human Rights Watch](46530.md) has stated that "Hamas and Islamic Jihad are committing war crimes by holding scores of Israelis and others as hostages in Gaza". They also added that "Civilians, including children, people with disabilities, and older people, should never be treated as bargaining chips", and "The armed groups should immediately and safely release all civilians detained".[^cite_note-370] The secretary-general of [Amnesty International](18947898.md) urged the immediate release of "all civilians who were abducted, including children", and supported an investigation into these incidents "as part of the International Criminal Court's ongoing investigation into crimes committed by all parties in the current conflict".[^cite_note-amnesty20231012-371]

During the Hamas attack approximately 200 people were taken hostage by militants.[^cite_note-372][^cite_note-theatlantic20231009-358] Hostages included women, children, elderly and even babies.[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375] Under the [Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva_Convention), taking hostages is described as a "grave breach".[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-warcrimes-wp-377] In response to Israeli airstrikes on civilian residences, Hamas threatened on 13 October to execute a hostage every time Israel bombed a home without giving advance warning.[^cite_note-378] Omar Shakir, Israel and Palestine director of Human Rights Watch, asserted hostage-taking was a "heinous crime" with no justification.[^cite_note-HRW11-13] [Amnesty International](18947898.md) noted the militant groups' actions — including hostage-taking — "flagrantly violated international law".[^cite_note-amnesty20231012-371]

#### Murder of hostages

On 31 August 2024, the IDF recovered the bodies of six hostages kidnapped during the Nova festival massacre, including [Hersh Goldberg-Polin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hersh_Goldberg-Polin), from a tunnel in Rafah. Autopsies revealed that they had been killed from close range just 1–2 days earlier. Subsequently, it was reported that Hamas militants holding Israeli hostages in Gaza were given new orders to execute them if Israeli forces approached.[^cite_note-379] Following the recovery and burial of the hostages, Hamas released a propaganda video showing one of the slain captives before her death, seemingly intended to inflict psychological distress on the families of the hostages. Additionally, Hamas issued a warning that it would execute any remaining hostages if Israel attempted a rescue operation.[^cite_note-380]

#### Killing of surrendered people

See also: [Nahal Oz attack](75068288.md)

### Perfidy

#### Human shields

See also: [Use of human shields by Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Use_of_human_shields_by_Hamas)

The [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) allege that Hamas uses civilians as human shields.[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] This allegation has received support from the [UK](31717.md), the [US](3434750.md), [Australia](4689264.md), and [European Commission](9974.md).[^cite_note-383] The Israeli army further argued the militant groups' hostages were being used as human shields.[^cite_note-warcrimes-wp-377] Human Rights Watch noted that using hostages, or any other person held in custody, as human shields is illegal.[^cite_note-hrw1-369] On the counter side, Israeli Defense Forces have been accused of making no, or an inadequate distinction between Hamas forces and civilians.[^cite_note-384]

All combatants, including insurgents, are bound by the law of war. [Louis René Beres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Ren%C3%A9_Beres) has analyzed the placing of military assets amid Palestinian civilian populations, in previous anti-terror wars against Hamas, categorizing this as a clear and punishable crime under international law. The act of [perfidy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perfidy) is described as a "grave breach" in Article 147 of the [Fourth Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Geneva_Convention). Placing military assets or personnel in civilian populated areas is forbidden by the [Hague Regulations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hague_Regulations).[^cite_note-baed-350] [Neve Gordon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neve_Gordon), professor of [international law](8195726.md) and [human rights](13831.md) at [Queen Mary University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Mary_University) of [London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_London) and co-author of the 2020 book *Human Shields: A History of People in the Line of Fire*,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGordonPerugini2020\[\[Category\:Wikipedia_articles_needing_page_number_citations_from_August_2024\]\]<sup_class="noprint_Inline-Template_"_style="white-space\:nowrap;">\[<i>\[\[Wikipedia\:Citing_sources|<span_title="This_citation_requires_a_reference_to_the_specific_page_or_range_of_pages_in_which_the_material_appears. (August_2024)">page needed</span>\]\]</i>\]</sup>-385] has stated that the Israeli military and government's claims of Hamas using Palestinian civilians as human shields "should be understood as a pre-emptive legal defence against accusations that Israel is committing war crimes and crimes against humanity in Gaza."[^cite_note-\:0-386] [Janina Dill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janina_Dill), a laws of war professor at [University of Oxford](31797.md), stated, "Even if Hamas uses civilians as human shields, those civilians are entitled to full protection under international law unless they directly participate in the fighting".[^cite_note-387]

#### Disguising military units

Hamas militants allegedly disguise themselves as civilians and hide weapons in schools, mosques and hospitals, which are violations of international law.[^cite_note-388] The Rome Statute and the Geneva Conventions article 37, require distinguishing combatants from civilians, and providing for medical treatment of the wounded by designated units.[^cite_note-389][^cite_note-apnews20231017-4][^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391]

#### Medical facilities



Map released by IDF Spokesperson's Unit alleging military use of the [al-Shifa Hospital](21218880.md) compound

Further information: [Alleged military use of al-Shifa hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alleged_military_use_of_al-Shifa_hospital) and [Al-Rantisi Hospital § Claims of military use](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Rantisi_Hospital#Claims_of_military_use)

During wartime, medical facilities are considered protected objects, and the use of them for military purposes is a war crime. Hamas has been condemned by the EU for using "hospitals and civilians as human shields".[^cite_note-392] Israel alleges it operates a major [command and control](7092305.md) center beneath Al-Shiba hospital. According to Amer Abu Awash, a member of Hamas' elite [Nukhba Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nukhba_Force), much of Hamas' underground infrastructure is beneath hospitals, placed there so that Israel "won't strike them".[^cite_note-393][^cite_note-How_Hamas_uses_hospitals-174] Abu Awash made this claim while handcuffed and undergoing IDF [interrogation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interrogation).[^cite_note-How_Hamas_uses_hospitals-174]

Israel's claims have been contradicted by medical staff and by some international bodies.[^cite_note-394] [Ghassan Abu-Sittah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghassan_Abu-Sittah), a resident doctor at al-Shifa, called Israel's assertion of Hamas using the hospital an "outlandish excuse" to bomb it.[^cite_note-sky-395] In response to the IDF allegations, Norwegian doctor [Mads Gilbert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mads_Gilbert), who has worked both at al-Shifa and al-Quds, said that Israel had a well-documented history of attacking civilian healthcare facilities without ever providing real evidence they were used in military capacities.[^cite_note-396]

The IDF claimed they found bullets, grenades, and an assortment of other small weapons at the [Al-Shifa Hospital](21218880.md),[^cite_note-397] and other hospitals in the Gaza Strip. However, the majority of what the IDF have shown is explicitly allowed to be present at a hospital in an urban war zone, according to the 1949 [Geneva Conventions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva_Conventions) - Convention(I), Article 22, Paragraph 3 - because small arms and ammunition can arrive with wounded combatants and may be difficult to dispose of in a timely manner.[^cite_note-Article_22_-_quote_par_3-398][^cite_note-Paragraph_3_-_2016-399] Combatants and civilians are allowed to be treated at the same shared facilities,[^cite_note-Article_22_-_paragraph_5-400] and both groups of patients are protected. The presence of small arms alone would not disqualify the hospitals from protected status as medical facilities,[^cite_note-Article_22_-_quote_par_3-398] and it would remain illegal to attack the hospitals or to obstruct medical staff doing their duties.[^cite_note-Article_19-401]

On 5 November, during the [invasion of Gaza](74998519.md), the IDF released videos claiming Hamas fighters fired from the [Sheikh Hamad Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Hamad_Hospital?action=edit\&redlink=1). In response, [Qatari official](25190.md) Mohammed El Emadi condemned Israel's claim, stating it was made "without concrete evidence or an independent investigation" and was a "blatant attempt to justify the occupation's targeting of civilian facilities."[^cite_note-402] The IDF video also showed an entrance to an underground complex, which according to the IDF led to the Hamas tunnel network.[^cite_note-403] An *[Al-Jazeera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Jazeera)* investigation found that the claimed "tunnel entrance" was instead a hatch for the hospital's water reservoir, which has existed since the hospital's construction.[^cite_note-404] In response to Israel's allegations, Hamas requested the [United Nations](31769.md) to form an international committee to inspect hospitals and verify what it called Israel's "false claims" that it uses them to fire rockets.[^cite_note-405]

On 14 November, CNN visited what Israel described as a Hamas commander's house, situated between a school and a hospital, and saw a tunnel beneath it that Israel said led in one direction to the school, and in the other direction to the hospital. Visiting the hospital, Israel showed weapons that they said they found in an armory there, as well as unconfirmed indications that hostages may have been held there.[^cite_note-CNNHospitalSchoolTunnel-406] According to *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, the origins of the weapons shown in the video could not be independently verified.[^cite_note-407] Charles Lister, Director of the Counterterrorism and Extremism Program at the [Middle East Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_Institute), stated the IDF footage clearly indicated that the basement was a [bomb shelter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bomb_shelter).[^cite_note-408] Mohammed Zaqout, a [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md) official responsible for Gaza's hospitals, stated the basement was a shelter for women and children.[^cite_note-409]

In December 2023, *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* analyzed the publicly released material by Israel, along with satellite imagery and other publicly available material, and concluded the rooms connected to a tunnel network did not show any evidence of being used by Hamas, and that each of the buildings IDF spokesman [Daniel Hagari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Hagari) had identified as being "directly involved" in Hamas's military activity did not appear to be connected to any tunnel network. *The Post* stated there was no evidence released that showed a tunnel network could be accessed from the hospital wards.[^cite_note-410]

In February 2024, *[The New York Times](30680.md)* analyzed the evidence, concluding that the tunnel likely connected to the broader tunnel network and that it could be accessed from buildings within the hospital complex, although not the individual wards.[^cite_note-411]

### Indiscriminate rocket attacks



Aftermath of Hamas rocket hit on the maternity ward of [Barzilai Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barzilai_Medical_Center), a hospital in southern Israel, during the Hamas-led attack on Israel

[^cite_note-412]

Further information: [List of Palestinian rocket attacks on Israel in 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_rocket_attacks_on_Israel_in_2023)

The 7 October attack included at least 4,300 rockets aimed toward Israel,[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414] with over 8000 being fired since the war began.[^cite_note-415][^cite_note-scmp8oct-416] The rockets hit as far away as [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and the outskirts of [Jerusalem](16043.md).[^cite_note-417] Human Rights Watch termed the rocket attacks as indiscriminate.[^cite_note-HRW11-13][^cite_note-johnhopkins-418] These have included repeated direct strikes on medical facilities, such as the Barzilai Medical Center in [Ashkelon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashkelon), including pediatric facilities at the [Child Development Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_Development_Institute?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-419] Human right organizations and scholars have condemned indiscriminate rocket attacks as a war crime.[^cite_note-johnhopkins-418][^cite_note-420]

### Sexual violence and abuse

This section is an excerpt from [Sexual and gender-based violence in the October 7 attacks](75436869.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks\&action=edit)]

During the [October 7 attacks](74998317.md), Israeli women, girls and men were reportedly subjected to [sexual violence](731637.md), including [rape](7980471.md) and [sexual assault](218407.md), by [Hamas](13913.md) or other Gazan militants.[^cite_note-421] The militants involved in the attack are accused of having committed acts of [gender-based violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender-related_violence), war crimes and [crimes against humanity](61099.md).[^cite_note-422][^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_BBC_News-2023-423][^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_PressUN2024-424] Hamas has denied that its fighters engaged in sexual violence[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERubin2023-425] and has called for an impartial international investigation into the accusations.[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_cnn20231210-427]

The extent of sexual violence perpetrated by militants, and whether it was planned and weaponised by the attackers, has been the subject of intense debate and controversy.[^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_ajefalseclaims-428][^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_PhilpWeiniger-429][^cite_note-430] Initially said to be "dozens" by Israeli authorities, they later clarified they could not provide a number.[^cite_note-431] In January 2024, it was reported that several victims of sexual violence from 7 October and captivity in Gaza had come forward.[^cite_note-432][^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_d2survivor-433] A number of initial testimonies of sexual violence were later discredited,[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_zaka-problems-435][^cite_note-436] while Israel has accused international human rights groups of downplaying assault reports.[^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_new_signs-437] As of January 2025, the former head of the security cases division in Israel's Southern District prosecutor's office said that no case was being filed due to a lack of evidence and complainants, which she said could be due to victims being dead or unwilling to come forward.[^cite_note-438]

The UN's [Special Representative on Sexual Violence in Conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_Special_Representative_of_the_Secretary-General_on_Sexual_Violence_in_Conflict), [Pramila Patten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pramila_Patten), reported in March 2024, with the "full cooperation" of the Israeli government,[^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_Voice_of_America-439] that there was "clear and convincing information" that [Israeli hostages in Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_hostages_in_Gaza) had experienced sexual violence,[^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_Borger-440][^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_likely-441] and that there was "reasonable grounds to believe that conflict-related sexual violence occurred during the 7 October attacks".[^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_PressUN2024-424][^cite_note-442][^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_Borger-440] The report was not a full investigation, but designed to "collect and verify allegations", and the team stated that their conclusions fell below the legal threshold of being "beyond a [reasonable doubt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reasonable_doubt)".[^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_PhilpWeiniger-429] In June 2024, the UN [Commission of Inquiry](69971286.md) (CoI) published a legally mandated report that stated there was "a pattern indicative of sexual violence by Palestinian forces during the attack", but that it was unable to independently verify allegations of rape due to Israel's obstruction of its investigation. It also found some of the allegations to be false and found "no credible evidence" that Palestinian militants had received direct orders to commit sexual violence.[^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_OHCHR-443]

On 12 April 2024, the [European Union](9317.md) sanctioned military and special forces wings of Hamas and the armed wing of [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](13093253.md) due to their responsibility for the alleged sexual violence on 7 October.[^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_Reuters120424-444] The EU said the two groups' fighters "committed widespread sexual and gender-based violence in a systematic manner, using it as a weapon of war."[^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_AA120424-445] On 23 April 2024, the annual UN Secretary-General's report called on the Israeli government to allow access to "relevant UN bodies to carry out a fully-fledged investigation into all alleged violations".[^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_UN_2023_annual_report-446][^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_jpost20240424-447][^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_timesofisrael20240423-448][^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_ynetnews20240423-449] In July 2025, Hamas was added to the UN's sexual violence blacklist.[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_October_7_attacks_UN_2024-451]

### Use of children

On 6 January 2024 Israeli military accused Hamas of using child soldiers, including to deliver explosive devices and to scout the battlefield in order to "assess the damage" on battlefields. They added that Hamas and Islamic Jihad had for years run summer camps in the Gaza Strip where children underwent military training.[^cite_note-IDF_child-452] Previously Ynet had reported that "a senior Hamas militant" captured by Israel had testified during interrogation that Hamas used children to carry explosives.[^cite_note-453][^cite_note-HorribleAu-454]

The use and recruitment of children under 15 as soldiers is a war crime.[^cite_note-HorribleAu-454]  But the IDF did not specify an age for the alleged child soldiers.[^cite_note-IDF_child-452]

### Looting



Palestinian irregulars looting stores in Israel during the 7 October attacks

[Looting](209811.md) took place in several kibbutzim and at Nova festival.[^cite_note-455][^cite_note-456] Videos published by the Times of Israel showed that the looting in kibbutz Be'eri was done by Palestinian civilians, who arrived at the kibbutz after the Hamas militants, stealing agricultural equipment, motorbikes, and televisions.[^cite_note-457]

### Attacks on civilian shipping

Main article: [Red Sea crisis](75207766.md)

As part of their [participation in the Gaza war](75207766.md) the [Houthi](17240332.md) militant group that rules part of Yemen has launched attacks against four civilian ships, and captured the *[Galaxy Leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galaxy_Leader)* and detained its crew. According to Human Rights Watch, such attacks and the taking of hostages constitute war crimes "if carried out deliberately or recklessly".[^cite_note-458]

## Allegations of genocide

### Against Israel

Main articles: [Gaza genocide](75668427.md) and [South Africa's genocide case against Israel](75736183.md)

Further information: [Palestinian genocide accusation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_accusation) and [Israeli war crimes § Genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_war_crimes#Genocide)

Israel has been accused of committing genocide against Palestinians in Gaza during the war.

The Israeli defence minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant) declared on 9 October: "we are fighting human animals".[^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460] As mentioned by *[the Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times)*, Gallant promised "a complete siege" on Gaza, with "no electricity, no food, no fuel". The next day he told Israeli troops he had "released all restraints". Giora Eiland, a former Israeli general, wrote the same month: "To make the siege effective, we have to prevent others from giving assistance to Gaza."[^cite_note-461]

On 15 October, [Third World approaches to international law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_World_approaches_to_international_law) published a statement signed by over 800 legal scholars expressing "alarm about the [possibility of the crime of genocide being perpetrated by Israeli forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_accusation) against Palestinians in the Gaza Strip".[^cite_note-462] On 17 October, [Genocide Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_Watch) declared a "Genocide Emergency Alert" for the conflict in Gaza.[^cite_note-Watch-2023-463] Several scholars have cited numerous Israeli statements that they argue constitute an "intent to destroy" the population of Gaza, a necessary condition for the legal threshold of genocide to be met.[^cite_note-464] On 16 November, [Office of the United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights](22463.md) (OHCHR) experts said that Israel's violations "point to a genocide in the making".[^cite_note-465] Several world leaders have accused Israel of genocide, including [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md) of [Brazil](3383.md) and [Gustavo Petro](13391962.md) of [Colombia](5222.md).[^cite_note-466][^cite_note-467] President [Hage Geingob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hage_Geingob) of Namibia called the actions of the Israeli government "genocidal and gruesome".[^cite_note-468]

On 29 December, [South Africa](17416221.md) submitted [a case](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_v._Israel_\(Genocide_Convention\)) to the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) (ICJ) alleging that Israel was committing genocide in Gaza and requested provisional measures for Israel to suspend its military campaign in Gaza.[^cite_note-Roelf-2023-6][^cite_note-469][^cite_note-470] In its filing, South Africa alleged that Israel's actions were "intended to bring about the destruction of a substantial part of the Palestinian national, racial and ethnical group".[^cite_note-471] South Africa's case was backed by [Belgium](3343.md), [Ireland](14560.md) and [Spain](26667.md) in Europe,[^cite_note-472] the [Organization of Islamic Cooperation](47725.md), as well as the [Arab League](52625.md). [Malaysia](3607937.md), [Namibia](21292.md) and [Pakistan](23235.md) independently backed the case.[^cite_note-473] The ICJ issued an Order in relation to the provisional measures request on 26 January 2024 in which it ordered Israel to take all measures to prevent any acts that could be considered genocidal but stopped short of calling for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-474] The interim judgement was interpreted that the ICJ had determined that it was "plausible" that some of Israel's actions may violate the Genocide Convention, however, [Joan Donoghue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joan_Donoghue), the president of the ICJ at the time of that ruling, corrected in an interview that this was not what the court had ruled, rather that Palestinians had "plausible rights to protection from genocide".[^cite_note-475] The final ruling is expected to take years.[^cite_note-476]

Some researchers in the fields of urban planning and architecture have alleged that the destruction in Gaza, particularly the destruction of historic buildings and essential infrastructure such as hospitals and universities, amounts to [domicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domicide), or [urbicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbicide).[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478][^cite_note-thenation20240124-103][^cite_note-479][^cite_note-480] In June 2024, the [IDF](39237.md) reported that their own analysis of [digital aerial mapping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerial_mapping) shows that only 16% of Gaza's buildings have been destroyed during the war.[^cite_note-481]

### Against Palestinian militants

Main article: [Allegations of genocide in the October 7 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_October_7_attacks)

Several experts in international law and [genocide studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_studies) characterized Hamas' assault as genocide.[^cite_note-Watch-2023-463][^cite_note-Winer2023-356][^cite_note-Haaretz-482] Legal and genocide experts have condemned the attack, during which 1,139 people were killed, including 695 Israeli civilians. They argue that these actions by Hamas constitute a significant violation of international law and were carried out with the intent to destroy the Israeli national group.[^cite_note-Haaretz-482][^cite_note-Winer2023-356][^cite_note-Watch-2023-463] Some commentators have pointed toward [Hamas's founding charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Hamas_charter), which contains [antisemitic](1078.md) language, [advocates for the destruction of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calls_for_the_destruction_of_Israel), and, according to some of the researchers, implies a call for the genocide of Jews. This has led to suggestions that the 7 October attacks were an effort to fulfill this agenda.[^cite_note-The_Economist-483][^cite_note-Hoffman-484][^cite_note-485][^cite_note-486][^cite_note-487]

In a 12 October preliminary legal assessment condemning Hamas's attacks in Israel, international humanitarian law scholar and Dean of [Cornell Law School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornell_Law_School) [Jens David Ohlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_David_Ohlin) said the evidence suggested Hamas's "killings and kidnappings" potentially violated Articles 6–8 of the [Rome Statute](68762.md) as well as the [Genocide Convention](143988.md) and were "crimes against humanity"; over a hundred international scholars expressed support for this position.[^cite_note-Winer2023-356][^cite_note-OJ-Ohlin-351]

In the West, Hamas has long been considered to harbor aspirations of genocide against Israel and its Jewish population, as an interpretation of their founding charter referenced a [hadith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hadith) (saying attributed to [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad)) that in the End Times, Jews will follow [the false Messiah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Masih_ad-Dajjal), after which Muslims will kill and be victorious over Jews.[^cite_note-488][^cite_note-489][^cite_note-490][^cite_note-491][^cite_note-492] Legal and genocide experts condemned the attack as a severe violation of international law, asserting that Hamas executed these acts with the intent to destroy the Israeli national group.[^cite_note-Haaretz-482][^cite_note-Winer2023-356][^cite_note-493][^cite_note-Watch-2023-463] Over 100 international scholars describing the actions as likely meeting the definition of genocide, saying "As these widespread, horrendous acts appear to have been carried out with an 'intent to destroy, in whole or in part' a national group – Israelis – a goal explicitly declared by Hamas, they most probably constitute an international crime of genocide, proscribed by the Genocide Convention and the Rome Statute of the International Criminal Court".[^cite_note-Winer2023-356]

Other groups, including [Genocide Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_Watch), have supported these allegations, saying "Hamas targeted Israelis simply because they were Israelis. It was the deadliest recorded massacre of Jews since the Holocaust. Iran, Hamas, Hezbollah, and Palestinian Islamic Jihad have expressed their genocidal intent to destroy the nation of Israel. The massacres by Hamas constituted acts of genocide."[^cite_note-Watch-2023-463]

## Legal proceedings

Various legal proceedings and sanctions have occurred, including two legal cases the *[South Africa's genocide case against Israel](75736183.md)* and *[Nicaragua v. Germany](76228006.md).* The [International Criminal Court](14880.md) has also set out arrest warrants of those accused of war crimes.[^cite_note-494]

**September 2025**

Two United States attorneys submitted an Article 15 communication to the International Criminal Court alleging that Iran’s Supreme Leader [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) and Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps commander [Esmail Qaani](62727035.md) committed war crimes, crimes against humanity and genocide for materially supporting Hamas’s 7 October 2023 attacks on Israel. The filing requests arrest warrants and an ICC investigation, asserting that Tehran supplied weapons, training and strategic direction that enabled the assault. Iran is not a party to the Rome Statute, and the Court had not announced any decision on the complaint at the time of the report.[^cite_note-495]
